as a MBE, a business must be a for-profit entity located in the United States, 51% owned, operated, capitalized and controlled by a member(s) of a presumed group, with the top executive officer {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Lw4zpz6YCPgALGLDY9bnLg","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Astronaut, LLC — Product Research\n     2→\n     3→Researched: 2026-02-14\n     4→Developer website: https://astronautehr.com\n     5→\n     6→## Overview\n     7→\n     8→Astronaut, LLC is a small, privately held EHR company founded in 2009 by Dr. Ignacio Valdes, a Houston-based psychiatrist board-certified in both Psychiatry and Clinical Informatics. The company builds **Astronaut EHR**, a cloud-hosted adaptation of **VistA** — the open-source electronic health record system originally developed by the U.S. Department of Veterans Affairs. Astronaut was the first VistA-based EHR to be deployed in the cloud. The company appears to be a very small operation: the leadership page lists a CEO (Dr. Valdes), a President (Janet Blazek Valdes), two senior software engineers, and a Director of Training. The developer contact email is a Gmail address.\n     9→\n    10→Dr. Valdes also operates Blue Bonnet Clinic, a psychiatry practice in Houston, which uses Astronaut EHR — making this both a product company and a practitioner-driven venture. The company markets itself as a low-cost alternative to proprietary EHR systems, emphasizing that implementation costs are \"a small fraction\" of commercial systems. Astronaut has a national footprint but appears to serve a small number of practices, primarily small ambulatory clinics and community health centers.\n    11→\n    12→## Product: Astronaut\n    13→\n    14→CHPL IDs: 10809\n    15→\n    16→### What It Is\n    17→\n    18→Astronaut EHR is a cloud-based, modernized version of **VistA** (Veterans Health Information Systems and Technology Architecture) with a customized version of **CPRS** (Computerized Patient Record System) — the standard VistA GUI client — rebranded as **Astro-CPRS**. The product builds on VistA's comprehensive clinical platform while adding proprietary features and modern web technologies to the user interface.\n    19→\n    20→The certified product (version 1709, certified 2022-02-01) carries a substantial set of ONC certifications — 30+ criteria spanning clinical data (a)(1)–(a)(14), care coordination (b)(1)/(b)(3), FHIR APIs (g)(10), Clinical Quality Measures (c)(1), public health (h)(1), and the EHI export requirement (b)(10). This broad certification footprint is consistent with VistA's heritage as a full-featured EHR.\n    21→\n    22→The product is browser-based (\"100% of browsers, no software installation required\") and cloud-delivered with automatic upgrades. It provides a CCDA Web API (version 2021.12.19) using HTTP Basic authentication for patient data exchange.\n    23→\n    24→### Users & Market\n    25→\n    26→**Target users:** Physicians, nurse practitioners, mid-level providers, and clinical staff in small-to-mid-size ambulatory practices. Dr. Valdes's own psychiatry practice (Blue Bonnet Clinic in Houston) is a reference deployment.\n    27→\n    28→**Settings:** The product has been deployed in individual physician offices, outpatient clinics, community health centers, and Partial Hospitalization Programs (PHPs). One announced customer is MSP-Healthcare, an Arizona-based provider who joined the Astronaut network in 2016.\n    29→\n    30→**Market position:** This is a very small, niche vendor. No customer counts, revenue figures, or market share data were found. The company does not appear on major EHR review sites (G2, Capterra, KLAS). No third-party reviews were found. The product is positioned as a low-cost option for practices that want VistA's clinical depth without a large IT footprint.\n    31→\n    32→**Go-to-market:** Direct sales. The company describes itself as a \"full-service EHR vendor\" offering billers, developers, and programmers — suggesting they provide implementation and support services directly rather than through channel partners.\n    33→\n    34→### Modules & Functionality\n    35→\n    36→Because Astronaut is built on VistA, it inherits VistA's extensive module architecture. The following capabilities are documented through a combination of Astronaut's own website, the Open Health News profile, the mandatory disclosures page, and VistA's well-known architecture:\n    37→\n    38→**Core VistA/CPRS capabilities (inherited):**\n    39→- **Computerized Physician Order Entry (CPOE):** Ordering of medications, laboratory tests, radiology/imaging, diets, and procedures — confirmed by (a)(1)–(a)(3) certification\n    40→- **Problem List:** Patient diagnoses and problem tracking — (a)(6)\n    41→- **Medication List / Pharmacy:** Active and historical medications, drug-drug and drug-allergy interaction checking — (a)(1), (a)(4)\n    42→- **Allergy/Adverse Reaction Tracking:** Recording and alerting on medication allergies\n    43→- **Laboratory:** Lab test ordering and results management — national lab ordering capability noted\n    44→- **Radiology/Imaging:** Imaging order entry and results\n    45→- **Progress Notes / Clinical Documentation:** Via VistA's Text Integration Utilities (TIU) — hundreds of \"pre-built clinically proven templates\" per Astronaut's marketing\n    46→- **Vital Signs:** Recording and tracking\n    47→- **Consults:** Requesting, tracking, and completing specialty consultations\n    48→- **Scheduling:** Enterprise scheduling for outpatient appointments; Admission/Discharge/Transfer (ADT) functions also noted\n    49→- **Demographics:** Patient demographic data management — (a)(5) certification\n    50→- **Family Health History:** Confirmed by (a)(12) certification\n    51→- **Implantable Device List:** Confirmed by (a)(14) certification\n    52→- **Clinical Decision Support (CDS):** Drug interaction checks and alerts — (a)(2), (a)(4)\n    53→\n    54→**E-Prescribing:**\n    55→- Integrated e-prescribing through **Newcrop** and the **Surescripts** network\n    56→- **EPCS** (Electronic Prescribing of Controlled Substances) — Surescripts-certified\n    57→- This is a third-party integration with associated monthly costs (\"Monthly Newcrop and the Surescripts network charge\")\n    58→\n    59→**Astronaut proprietary additions:**\n    60→- **Rocket Note:** Described as \"very efficient follow-up notes and billing\" — suggesting integrated clinical documentation and billing code capture\n    61→- **Turbo Supervision:** Mid-level provider supervision tools with \"patient navigation capabilities\" — designed for attending physicians supervising NPs/PAs\n    62→- **Astro-CPRS:** Enhanced CPRS interface with modern web technologies, improved typography, embedded graphics, and single sign-on\n    63→\n    64→**Interoperability:**\n    65→- CCDA generation and exchange — (b)(1), (b)(3)\n    66→- FHIR R4 API — (g)(10) certified\n    67→- Patient access API — (e)(3) certified for patient electronic access to health information\n    68→- Direct messaging for transitions of care implied by (b)(1)/(b)(3)\n    69→\n    70→**Quality Reporting:**\n    71→- Supports 16 Clinical Quality Measures (CQMs) per the disclosures page — (c)(1) certified\n    72→- QRDA reporting capability (the disclosures mention potential \"hourly charge to assist training, preparing, and submitting QRQA to the government\")\n    73→\n    74→**Public Health:**\n    75→- Immunization registry transmission — (h)(1) certified\n    76→\n    77→### Data & Content\n    78→\n {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01N2nf797zZ1qjiZyA7G5oqp","type":"tool_result","content":[{"type":"text","text":"Here is the comprehensive research report on Brilogy Corporation and AXEIUM EHR.\n\n---\n\n## Brilogy Corporation / AXEIUM -- Research Findings\n\n### URLs Visited and What Was Found\n\n**1. https://www.axeium.com** -- Main product website. Rich content describing AXEIUM as \"the one and only practice management and electronic health records system that supports all Community Health Service Lines.\" Lists features across Medical, Dental, Vision, and Behavioral Health modules. Describes the grant-funded consortium origins. Lists participating community health centers. No pricing on this page.\n\n**2. http://www.axeium.com/Certifications** -- Mandatory disclosures page. Contains full ONC certification details (certificate number 15.05.05.1171.BRIL.02.01.1.221219, certified 2022-12-19, last updated 2024-12-27), all 26+ certified criteria, API documentation with FHIR endpoints, pricing model disclosure (hybrid client-hosted with monthly subscription + visit-based SaaS fee), required third-party software (MDToolbox for Direct messaging, Paubox for secure email), and cost breakdowns.\n\n**3. https://www.brilogy.com** -- Connection failed/socket closed. The brilogy.com domain appears to be down or redirecting. However, the LA City WBE certification listing (https://bca.lacity.gov/CertificationListings/CoDetail.php?id=10893) confirmed Brilogy Corporation's business categories: \"Computer Systems Design Services / Custom Computer Programming Services / Employment Placement Agencies / Temporary Help Services.\" WBE certified since 2007, owned by Julie Allione.\n\n**4. https://axeium.com/Dental, /Vision, /Behavior, /Medical** -- Detailed module-specific pages with granular feature descriptions for each specialty service line.\n\n**5. https://axeium.com/EHI** -- EHI export documentation confirming tab-separated file format using native record layout, with separate handling for rich text documents and images.\n\n**6. https://axeium.com/Certifications/Community-Health-Certified-Lab-Interfaces** -- Certified interfaces with Quest Diagnostics, LabCorp (ICD-10 certified), and Meditech (custom integration for hospital-sponsored lab systems).\n\n**7. https://axeium.com/Testimonials** -- 10 testimonials from community health center staff across Southern California.\n\n**8. https://axeium.com/RWT** -- Real World Testing results posted annually since 2022, covering Application Access, CQMs, Direct Messaging, Transition of Care, and VDT.\n\n**9. https://www.emrsystems.net/axeium-emr-software/** -- Third-party review listing: 4/5 stars (2 reviews), base price $146/month, positive feedback on customization and dental charting.\n\n**10. https://www.emrfinder.com/axeium-community-health-emr-software/** -- Third-party review listing: 4/5 stars (4 reviews). Reviewers from South Boston Community Health Center, LBU Community Clinic, Hazelton Medical Practice, and North Florida Medical Associates. Positive on integrations and mobile accessibility.\n\n**11. https://www.brookhollowbusinesspark.com/?business_directory=axeium-brilogy** -- Office park directory confirming Suite 250, 1528 Brookhollow Drive, Santa Ana, CA.\n\n---\n\n### What Kind of Product Is AXEIUM?\n\nAXEIUM is a **full-featured, multi-specialty EHR and Practice Management system purpose-built for Federally Qualified Health Centers (FQHCs) and Community Health Centers (CHCs)**. It is classified by ONC as a \"Modular EHR\" for the Ambulatory practice setting, but in practice it functions as a comprehensive, integrated platform covering clinical, administrative, and financial operations across multiple service lines.\n\nThis is not a general-purpose EHR or a single-specialty product. It was designed from the ground up for the unique requirements of safety-net healthcare organizations that deliver medical, dental, vision, and behavioral health services under one organizational umbrella. The product positions itself as \"the one and only practice management and electronic health records system that supports all Community Health Service Lines.\"\n\nThe system uses a **hybrid, client-hosted deployment model** -- the data is stored on the client's own servers (\"It is your data that is stored on your servers\"), with a Windows desktop client as the primary interface and mobile/web extensions available. This is notable: it is not a pure cloud/SaaS product, though it has cloud-like characteristics (visit-based pricing, automatic updates).\n\n---\n\n### Who Uses It?\n\n**Primary market:** FQHCs, free clinics, and community health centers, predominantly in **Southern California / Orange County, CA**. The product originated from a decade-long grant-funded consortium supported by:\n- United Healthcare\n- Kaiser Permanente\n- Tides Foundation\n- Orange County Healthcare Agency\n- Orange County Community Foundation\n- Coalition of Orange County Community Health Centers\n\n**Known client organizations** (from testimonials and development partners):\n- La Amistad Family Health Center (Orange, CA)\n- The Gary Center\n- Share Our Selves (Costa Mesa, CA -- a nationally recognized FQHC)\n- Camino Health Center\n- Lestonnac Free Clinic\n- St. Joseph Hospital of Orange / St. Joseph Community Health\n- Puente Mobile Vision\n- CHOC Children's\n- St. Jude Neighborhood Health Centers\n\n**EMR review site reviewers** (suggesting broader geographic reach):\n- South Boston Community Health Center\n- LBU Community Clinic\n- Hazelton Medical Practice\n- North Florida Medical Associates\n\n**Practice size:** Small to mid-size community health organizations, typically multi-site with multiple service lines. The system is designed for \"high-volume clinics\" with features like patient photo capture at check-in, group class scheduling, and program tracking (e.g., food distribution programs).\n\n**Specialties served:**\n- Primary Care / Family Medicine\n- Dental (with full dental charting, odontograms, periodontal charts, digital x-ray interfaces)\n- Vision / Optometry (refraction, tonometry, anterior/posterior segment exams, gonioscopy)\n- Behavioral Health / Mental Health / Community Counseling (SIRP workflow, assessments, treatment plans, group therapy)\n- Pediatrics (indicated by CQMs for childhood immunization, fluoride varnish, dental decay)\n- Women's Health (indicated by CQMs for cervical cancer and breast cancer screening)\n\n---\n\n### Modules and Features\n\n**Clinical -- General Medical Module** (source: https://axeium.com/Medical):\n- Vitals collection with automated MA queue routing, dual BP readings, BMI auto-calculation, color-coded displays, trend charting\n- Customizable exam templates with configurable defaults, required fields, tab organization\n- Clinical Decision Support (CDS) rules triggering alerts based on conditions, demographics, and lab data\n- Problem list with 6000+ SNOMED conditions, ICD-9/10 cross-referencing, severity adjustment\n- Medication management with Lexicomp drug database, Surescripts ePrescribe (including eRefill), drug-drug and drug-allergy interaction checking, on/off-formulary notifications\n- Allergy tracking (drug, food, environmental) with RxNorm codes and CDS alerts\n- Laboratory integration with Quest Diagnostics, LabCorp, and Meditech -- fully integrated order entry, auto-send, auto-match results, abnormal flagging, on-the-fly graphing, LOINC-coded structured data\n- Immunization management with VIS in patient's preferred language, HL7 transmission to registries\n- Surveys and assessment builder converting paper forms to electronic\n- Health maintenance schedule with rules-based policy engine for condition-specific reminders\n- SOAP and progress notes with AutoText phrases and hot-key shortcuts\n- Electronic document repository with scanning, classification, task routing, batch scanning, multi-monitor support\n- Drawings and annotation on clinical images\n\n**Dental Module** (source: https://axeium.com/Dental):\n- Tooth charting with configurable gingivitis, prognosis\n- Treatment plans importing from charted work, multiple plans per patient, automatic status updates\n- Automatic CDT coding from completed work\n- Progress notes with AutoText segment documentation (retina, choroid, optic nerve)\n- Glaucoma assessment with tonometry history and gonioscopy\n- Medication management with cross-service interaction checking\n\n**Behavioral integration, auto-population of known patient data\n- SIRP workflow (Situation-Intervention-Response-Plan) with configurable templates and automatic clinical note generation\n- Treatment planning with long-term goals, action tracking, printable plans for client signatures\n- Quick-access buttons for patient education, referrals, diagnosis codes\n\n**Administrative / Practice Management:**\n- Patient management system for high-volume clinics\n- Appointment scheduling (next available, calendar, search by service/provider/location)\n- Provider schedule management\n- Group classes scheduling and check-in\n- Task assignment to users or roles\n- Patient contact logs (calls, correspondence, recalls)\n- Patient check-in/check-out with photo capture and fee collection\n- News management with authentication records\n- Emergency access records\n\n**Reporting data:**\n- CQM/eCQM data for 8 measures\n- UDS reporting data\n- OSHPD reporting data\n- Custom query results\n\n**EHI Export It is classified by ONC as a \"Modular EHR\" for the Ambulatory practice setting, but in practice it functions as a comprehensive, integrated platform covering clinical, administrative, and financial operations across multiple service lines.\n\nThis is not a general-purpose EHR or a single-specialty product. It was designed from the ground up for the unique requirements of safety-net healthcare organizations that deliver medical, dental, vision, and behavioral health services under one organizational umbrella. The product positions itself as \"the one and only practice management and electronic health records system that supports all Community Health Service Lines.\"\n\nThe system uses a **hybrid, client-hosted deployment model** -- the data is stored on the client's own servers (\"It is your data that is stored on your servers\"), with a Windows desktop client as the primary interface and mobile/web extensions available. like food distribution)\n\n**Clinical data -- Medical:**\n- Problem/diagnosis lists (SNOMED, ICD-9/ICD-10)\n- Medication lists (active and historical, via Lexicomp database)\n- Allergy records (drug/food/environmental, RxNorm coded, with severity)\n- Vital signs (with historical trending)\n- Lab orders and results (structured, LOINC-coded)\n- Immunization records (with VIS documentation, HL7 registry submissions)\n- Family health history\n- Implantable device records\n- Clinical exam data (from configurable templates)\n- Progress notes and SOAP notes\n- CDS alerts and clinical decision support data\n- Health maintenance schedules and reminders\n- Prescription history (via Surescripts/ePrescribe)\n\n**Clinical data -- Dental:**\n- Tooth charting and odontograms\n- Periodontal charting (probe depths, mobility, gingivitis/periodontitis, prognosis)\n- Treatment plans (planned and completed work, per-tooth history with provider and date)\n- CDT procedure codes\n- Dental imaging references (DEXIS, Planmeca Romexis)\n\n**Clinical data -- Vision:**\n- Refraction measurements (myopia, hyperopia, astigmatism)\n- Habitual and manifest refraction data\n- Final Rx history\n- Anterior and posterior segment findings\n- Tonometry (intraocular pressure) history\n- Gonioscopy documentation\n- Clinical drawings and annotations\n\n**Clinical data -- Behavioral Health:**\n- Intake assessments (with auto-scoring)\n- SIRP documentation (Situation-Intervention-Response-Plan)\n- Treatment plans with goals and actions\n- Assessment scores and histories\n- Group therapy/class attendance records\n\n**Administrative/financial data:**\n- Appointment scheduling data\n- Provider schedules\n- Check-in/check-out records\n- Fee collection records\n- Billing data (HCFA 1500, PM-160)\n- Superbill data (CPT/ICD codes)\n- Patient contact logs (calls, correspondence, recalls)\n- Task assignments\n\n**Documents and images:**\n- Scanned documents (classified in three-level taxonomy)\n- Clinical images and annotations\n- Referral documents\n- Lab result PDFs\n- Rich text documents\n\n**Interoperability data:**\n- C-CDA documents (sent and received)\n- Transition of care records\n- Clinical information reconciliation data (medications, allergies, problems reconciled from external sources)\n- Direct messages sent/received\n- FHIR API access logs\n\n**Audit and security data:**\n- Access control records\n- Audit logs (tamper-resistant, per (d)(2))\n- Audit reports\n- Amendment records\n- Accounting of disclosures\n- Multi-factor authentication records\n- Emergency access records\n\n**Reporting data:**\n- CQM/eCQM data for 8 measures\n- UDS reporting data\n- OSHPD reporting data\n- Custom query results\n\n**EHI Export format:** Tab-separated files using AXEIUM's native record layout, with a published schema (Schema.pdf). Non-tabular content (rich text documents and images) exported separately. The actual export scope varies by deployed applications, software version, external data sources, and site configuration.\n\n---\n\n### Research Gaps\n\n- The brilogy.com website was unreachable; the {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_01F7bZuCo8Gaek9NZ4EuE73Q","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01GZPi2intW7szufkgS4Ewwc","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/jmandel/hobby/ehi-export-analysis/results/brilogy-corporation/product-research.md","content":"# Brilogy Corporation — Product Research\n\nResearched: 2026-02-14\nDeveloper website: https://www.axeium.com\n\n## Overview\n\nBrilogy Corporation is a very small, regional IT services company based in Santa Ana, CA (Orange County), founded in 1996. The company is WBE-certified (Women Business Enterprise) through LA City since 2007, owned by Julie Allione (CEO), with Milton Allione serving as President. The company operates with roughly 11–50 employees and estimated revenue of $5M–$20M. Brilogy is a Microsoft Gold Certified Partner whose core business categories include computer systems design, custom programming, and staffing services — but its primary public-facing product is AXEIUM, a multi-specialty EHR and practice management system purpose-built for Federally Qualified Health Centers (FQHCs) and community health centers.\n\nAXEIUM originated from a decade-long grant-funded consortium effort supported by United Healthcare, Kaiser Permanente, Tides Foundation, Orange County Healthcare Agency, Orange County Community Foundation, and the Coalition of Orange County Community Health Centers. This public-good origin — rather than a typical commercial startup path — is distinctive and explains the product's deep specialization for safety-net healthcare organizations. The vendor has essentially no presence on major review platforms (G2, Capterra, KLAS), no press coverage, and no Crunchbase activity. The brilogy.com parent website was unreachable during research; the company appears to operate almost entirely under the AXEIUM brand for its EHR business. In the FQHC EHR market, AXEIUM competes against much larger players like eClinicalWorks, NextGen, athenahealth, and Netsmart, differentiating on its purpose-built multi-service-line integration.\n\n## Product: AXEIUM\n\nCHPL ID: 11086\n\n### What It Is\n\nAXEIUM is a full-featured, multi-specialty EHR and practice management system designed specifically for community health centers that deliver medical, dental, vision, and behavioral health services under one organizational umbrella. The vendor positions it as \"the one and only practice management and electronic health records system that supports all Community Health Service Lines.\" ONC classifies it as a Modular EHR for the Ambulatory setting, but in practice it functions as a comprehensive integrated platform covering clinical, administrative, and financial operations.\n\nThe certified module (CHPL 15.05.05.1171.BRIL.02.01.1.221219, certified 2022-12-19, version MU3) covers 26+ criteria spanning clinical data management, transitions of care, clinical quality measures, patient portal, FHIR APIs, and direct messaging. This is the product — the certified module and the full AXEIUM system appear to be one and the same, not a component of something larger.\n\nThe system uses a hybrid, client-hosted deployment model — data is stored on the client's own servers (\"It is your data that is stored on your servers\"), with a Windows desktop client as the primary interface and mobile/web extensions available. Pricing disclosed on the certifications page includes a monthly subscription plus visit-based SaaS fee.\n\n### Users & Market\n\n**Primary users:** FQHCs, free clinics, and community health centers, predominantly in Southern California / Orange County. The product's grant-funded consortium origins tied it closely to the Orange County community health ecosystem.\n\n**Known client organizations** (from testimonials and development history):\n- La Amistad Family Health Center (Orange, CA)\n- The Gary Center\n- Share Our Selves (Costa Mesa, CA — a nationally recognized FQHC)\n- Camino Health Center\n- Lestonnac Free Clinic\n- St. Joseph Hospital of Orange / St. Joseph Community Health\n- Puente Mobile Vision\n- CHOC Children's\n- St. Jude Neighborhood Health Centers\n\nEMR review site reviewers suggest some geographic reach beyond Southern California (South Boston Community Health Center, North Florida Medical Associates), though the total number of deployed sites is unknown. Third-party reviews on small EMR directory sites (EMRSystems.net: 2 reviews, 4/5 stars; EMRFinder.com: 4 reviews, 4/5 stars) are positive but very sparse.\n\n**Day-to-day users:** Physicians, medical assistants, dentists, dental hygienists, optometrists, behavioral health counselors, front desk/check-in staff, billing staff, and practice managers. The system is designed for high-volume clinics with features like patient photo capture at check-in, group class scheduling, and program tracking.\n\n**Settings:** Multi-service-line community health centers offering primary care, dental, vision, and behavioral health. Designed for small to mid-size organizations, typically multi-site.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nAXEIUM's module structure is organized around four clinical service lines plus administrative/practice management capabilities. The following is drawn from the vendor's own module pages.\n\n**Medical Module** (source: axeium.com/Medical):\n- Vitals collection with automated MA queue routing, dual BP readings, BMI auto-calculation, color-coded displays, trend charting\n- Customizable exam templates with configurable defaults, required fields, tab organization\n- Clinical Decision Support (CDS) rules triggering alerts based on conditions, demographics, and lab data\n- Problem list with 6000+ SNOMED conditions, ICD-9/10 cross-referencing, severity adjustment\n- Medication management with Lexicomp drug database, Surescripts ePrescribe (including eRefill), drug-drug and drug-allergy interaction checking, on/off-formulary notifications\n- Allergy tracking (drug, food, environmental) with RxNorm codes and CDS alerts\n- Lab integration with Quest Diagnostics, LabCorp, and Meditech — fully integrated order entry, auto-send, auto-match results, abnormal flagging, on-the-fly graphing, LOINC-coded structured data\n- Immunization management with VIS in patient's preferred language, HL7 transmission to registries\n- Surveys and assessment builder converting paper forms to electronic\n- Health maintenance schedule with rules-based policy engine for condition-specific reminders\n- SOAP and progress notes with AutoText phrases and hot-key shortcuts\n- Electronic document repository with scanning, classification, task routing, batch scanning, multi-monitor support\n- Drawings and annotation on clinical images\n\n**Dental Module** (source: axeium.com/Dental):\n- Tooth charting with configurable QuickChart buttons, color-coded existing vs. planned work, graphic tooth surface picker\n- Periodontal charting with electronic probe depth tracking, mobility, periodontitis, gingivitis, prognosis\n- Treatment plans importing from charted work, multiple plans per patient, automatic status updates\n- Automatic CDT coding from completed work\n- Progress notes with AutoText templates\n- Imaging integration with DEXIS and Planmeca Romexis (bidirectional, automatic demographic sync)\n\n**Vision Module** (source: axeium.com/Vision):\n- Symptoms recording with customizable templates\n- Refraction and prescription with myopia/hyperopia/astigmatism tracking, historical Rx viewing, glasses prescription printing\n- Anterior and posterior segment documentation\n- Glaucoma assessment with tonometry history and gonioscopy\n- Clinical drawings and annotations\n\n**Behavioral Health Module** (source: axeium.com/Behavior):\n- Assessment management with scheduled reminders, patient alerts, automatic scoring\n- Customizable intake forms with Microsoft Office integration, auto-population of known patient data\n- SIRP workflow (Situation-Intervention-Response-Plan) with configurable templates and automatic clinical note generation\n- Treatment planning with long-term goals, action tracking, printable plans for client signatures\n\n**Administrative / Practice Management:**\n- Patient management for high-volume clinics\n- Appointment scheduling (next available, calendar, search by service/provider/location)\n- Provider schedule management\n- Group classes scheduling and check-in\n- Task assignment to users or roles\n- Patient contact logs (calls, correspondence, recalls)\n- Patient check-in/check-out with photo capture and fee collection\n- News management with read receipts\n\n**Billing:**\n- HCFA 1500 (standard commercial billing)\n- PM-160 (FQHC-specific billing)\n- Electronic billing\n- CPT and ICD code libraries, superbills\n\n**Reporting:**\n- 8 certified eCQMs: CMS2 (depression screening), CMS74 (fluoride varnish), CMS75 (childhood dental decay), CMS117 (childhood immunization), CMS122 (diabetes HbA1c), CMS124 (cervical cancer screening), CMS125 (breast cancer screening), CMS165 (blood pressure control)\n- OSHPD reporting (California state health facility reporting)\n- UDS reporting (HRSA Uniform Data System — mandatory for FQHCs)\n- Enterprise report writer with custom queries, filtering, Excel export, shareable views\n\n**Interoperability:**\n- RESTful FHIR API (patient search and patient data endpoints)\n- C-CDA document creation and exchange\n- Direct messaging via MDToolbox (Surescripts HISP) — required third-party component\n- Secure email via Paubox — required third-party component\n- Lab interfaces (Quest, LabCorp, Meditech)\n- Imaging interfaces (DEXIS, Planmeca Romexis)\n- eReferral network connecting health centers, hospitals, and surgery centers\n- CCD import/export with medication reconciliation\n- Patient portal with View/Download/Transmit capability\n- Mobile app (iOS and Android)\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the vendor's module descriptions, certification criteria, and EHI export documentation, AXEIUM stores the following categories of data:\n\n**Patient demographics:** Name, DOB, contact information (multiple addresses, phone numbers), insurance, employment, language, race/ethnicity, smoking status, poverty scale, patient photo, chart number, DIRECT address, program assignments (for grant/specialty programs like food distribution).\n\n**Medical clinical data:** Problem/diagnosis lists (SNOMED, ICD-9/ICD-10), medication lists (active and historical, via Lexicomp), allergy records (drug/food/environmental, RxNorm coded with severity), vital signs with historical trends, lab orders and results (structured, LOINC-coded), immunization records (with VIS documentation and HL7 registry submissions), family health history, implantable device records, clinical exam data from configurable templates, progress notes and SOAP notes, CDS alerts, health maintenance schedules, prescription history via Surescripts.\n\n**Dental clinical data:** Tooth charts and odontograms, periodontal charts (probe depths, mobility, gingivitis/periodontitis, prognosis), treatment plans (planned and completed per-tooth with provider and date), CDT procedure codes, dental imaging references.\n\n**Vision clinical data:** Refraction measurements, habitual and manifest refraction data, final Rx history, anterior and posterior segment findings, tonometry history, gonioscopy documentation, clinical drawings and annotations.\n\n**Behavioral health clinical data:** Intake assessments with auto-scoring, SIRP documentation, treatment plans with goals and actions, assessment score histories, group therapy/class attendance records.\n\n**Administrative/financial data:** Appointment scheduling data, provider schedules, check-in/check-out records, fee collection records, billing data (HCFA 1500, PM-160), superbill data, patient contact logs, task assignments.\n\n**Documents and images:** Scanned documents (classified in three-level taxonomy), clinical images and annotations, referral documents, lab result PDFs, rich text documents.\n\n**Interoperability/exchange data:** C-CDA documents (sent and received), transition of care records, reconciliation data (medications, allergies, problems reconciled from external sources), Direct messages.\n\n**Audit and compliance data:** Access control records, audit logs (tamper-resistant per (d)(2)), amendment records, accounting of disclosures, emergency access records.\n\n**Reporting data:** CQM/eCQM data for 8 measures, UDS reporting data, OSHPD reporting data.\n\nThe EHI export (per axeium.com/EHI) uses tab-separated files in AXEIUM's native record layout with a published schema (Schema.pdf), with separate handling for rich text documents and images. The actual export scope varies by deployed applications, software version, external data sources, and site configuration.\n\n---\n\n## Research Gaps\n\n- The brilogy.com website was unreachable; the parent company's full service portfolio and history could not be assessed directly.\n- No customer count or total deployment numbers are available.\n- AXEIUM does not appear on G2, Capterra, or KLAS — third-party validation is very limited.\n- No press releases, news articles, or media coverage were found about Brilogy Corporation.\n- The patient portal's specific features beyond ONC-required VDT are unclear.\n- The exact number of database tables/fields in the EHI export requires the Schema.pdf document from the EHI page (to be reviewed in Phase 2).\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":5611,"cache_read_input_tokens":35349,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":5611},"output_tokens":3,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"4166c405-feeb-4395-b2ec-cc2c73682c45","uuid":"2d117c72-bb5b-4cb4-9373-62815534cecd"}

clinical exam data from configurable templates, progress notes and SOAP notes, CDS alerts, health maintenance schedules, prescription history via Surescripts.\n\n**Dental clinical data:** Tooth charts and odontograms, periodontal charts (probe depths, mobility, gingivitis/periodontitis, prognosis), treatment plans (planned and completed per-tooth with provider and date), CDT procedure codes, dental imaging references.\n\n**Vision clinical data:** Refraction measurements, habitual and manifest refraction data, final Rx history, anterior and posterior segment findings, tonometry history, gonioscopy documentation, clinical drawings and annotations.\n\n**Behavioral health clinical data:** Intake assessments with auto-scoring, SIRP documentation, treatment plans with goals and actions, assessment score histories, group therapy/class attendance records.\n\n**Administrative/financial data:** Appointment scheduling data, provider schedules, check-in/check-out records, fee collection records, billing data (HCFA 1500, PM-160), superbill data, patient contact logs, task assignments.\n\n**Documents and images:** Scanned documents (classified in three-level taxonomy), clinical images and annotations, referral documents, lab result PDFs, rich text documents.\n\n**Interoperability/exchange data:** C-CDA documents (sent and received), transition of care records, reconciliation data (medications, allergies, problems reconciled from external sources), Direct messages.\n\n**Audit and compliance data:** Access control records, audit logs (tamper-resistant per (d)(2)), amendment records, accounting of disclosures, emergency access records.\n\n**Reporting data:** CQM/eCQM data for 8 measures, UDS reporting data, OSHPD reporting data.\n\nThe EHI export (per axeium.com/EHI) uses tab-separated files in AXEIUM's native record layout with a published schema (Schema.pdf), with separate handling for rich text documents and images. The actual export scope varies by deployed applications, software version, external data sources, and site configuration.\n\n---\n\n## Research Gaps\n\n- The brilogy.com website was unreachable; the parent company's full service portfolio and history could not be assessed directly.\n- No customer count or total deployment numbers are available.\n- AXEIUM does not appear on G2, Capterra, or KLAS — third-party validation is very limited.\n- No press releases, news articles, or media coverage were found about Brilogy Corporation.\n- The patient portal's specific features beyond ONC-required VDT are unclear.\n- The exact number of database tables/fields in the EHI export requires the Schema.pdf document from the EHI page (to be reviewed in Phase 2).\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
